# Copy changes: plain, direct wording

Branch `feat/plain-copy`, based on `main` at `0813699c5df8df78f1d6aef9e737aec029ef0b2b`.

## Summary

- **1223 unique string changes** across **121 files**. One row per unique before → after pair; where the same pair appears in several files, the files share a row.
- The owner's example sets the pattern: "Pickup included when we buy" now reads **"Free towing included"** on the hero, the steps, cards and titles, and **"Free towing when we buy"** / "Towing is free when we buy your car" wherever the sentence stands on its own (FAQ answers, steps, service and suburb copy, blog guides).
- Buttons and links that open the quote form all read **"Get my quote"** (was a mix of "Get my free quote", "Start your quote", "Request a quote", "Get an assessment", "Full quote").
- No prices, numbers, guarantees, review counts, licences, insurance or timing promises were added. Suburb, city and service keywords ("cash for cars", "car removal", "sell my car", Brisbane, Logan, Ipswich, Moreton Bay, Redlands and each suburb) are kept where they were.
- Dates (`date`, `updatedAt`, `reviewedAt`), slugs, URLs, link targets and heading anchors that other pages link to are unchanged.

| Home hero bullet | Pickup included when we buy | Free towing included |
| Home page title (browser tab and Google) | Cash for Cars Brisbane \| Written Offer & Pickup \| Caraway | Cash for Cars Brisbane \| Free Towing Included \| Caraway |
| Home hero bullet | Human-reviewed vehicle assessment | A person reviews your car |
| Home hero paragraph | Request an assessment in minutes. Caraway is a Brisbane-based car buyer: you get a written offer before anything is booked, pickup is included when we buy, and payment is confirmed at collection. | Tell us about your car. It takes a few minutes. You get a written offer before anything is booked. Towing is free when we buy, and payment is confirmed at pickup. |
| Quote form button (hero) | Get my free quote | Get my quote |
| "How it works" step 3 | We come to you: Pickup included when we buy. | We come to you: Free towing included. |
| Home badge band | Get an assessment / Pickup when we buy | Get my quote / Free towing when we buy |
| Car removal page title and H1 | Car Removal Brisbane \| Pickup Included When We Buy | Car Removal Brisbane \| Free Towing Included |
| FAQ question (home and /faq) | How are pickup costs handled? / Is pickup included? | Is towing free? ("Yes, towing is free when we buy your car and the car, location and access match what you told us.") |
| About page, "What sets us apart" | Pickup included when we buy. When the vehicle and access match the details supplied. | Free towing included. When we buy your car and the access matches what you told us. |

## Page titles

13 page titles changed. None is longer than before and every changed `title` field is 60 characters or fewer. 39 blog and resource pages render over 60 characters once the site adds " | Caraway" (41 on the live site before this change); those titles were already that long and were left as they are, apart from four that this change shortened.

## Wording left alone, and why

- **Terms of Service (`src/views/Terms.tsx`) and Privacy Policy (`src/views/Privacy.tsx`): not changed at all.** They are legal text. The Terms already say "Pickup is included when Caraway buys and the supplied vehicle, location, and access details match", which means the same as "free towing when we buy". The owner may want to use the word "towing" there as well; that is a legal edit with a new "last updated" date, so it is left for the owner to decide.
- **Consent and privacy notices on the forms and chat** ("By submitting, you agree we may contact you…", the marketing-consent checkbox, the chat privacy note): not changed.
- **Accessibility statement and the customer review pages** (`/accessibility`, `/review`, `/review/card`): not changed. The review pages use deliberately neutral, no-incentive wording.
- **Regulatory sentences in blog guides, the Queensland sale-record builder and the vehicle-data page** (what TMR, PPSR, councils, insurers or the ATO require; official statistics, sources and licence notes): kept word for word, or changed only where nothing legal changes.
- **Titles and H1s that carry a page's search phrase** (for example "Cash for Cars Brisbane, without the runaround.", "Vehicle Selling FAQs for Brisbane", "Scrap Car Removal in Brisbane", suburb H1s): kept. They are already short.
- **Internal emails** (`src/lib/quote-email.ts`, `src/lib/contact-email.ts`): these are lead notifications to the business inbox, not customer emails, so they are unchanged.
- **The 14 featured service guides** never say "free towing". The content check blocks that phrase there, so they say "towing is included when we buy your car".

## How the honesty checks were kept

- `scripts/check-content-integrity.mjs` still blocks prices, insurance and licence claims, same-day promises, "cash on the spot", and universal acceptance claims, unchanged.
- On the "truth consistency" files, a line that says towing or pickup is free or included must still say it applies when Caraway buys ("when we buy" / "when Caraway buys"). The allow pattern was shortened to that purchase condition so "Free towing when we buy" passes, and bare "Free towing included" still fails there.
- A new site-wide rule blocks "free towing regardless", "always free", "universally free" and similar.

| `app/how-it-works/page.tsx` | The assessment starts with the vehicle, condition, contact, and pickup details requested in the quote tool below, or you can call {BUSINESS.phoneDisplay}. Clear photos of the outside, the inside, the access and any damage help. Accurate details make it less likely the offer or pickup plan has to change later. |

| `app/how-it-works/page.tsx` | 3. Confirm the collection plan | 3. Book the pickup |
| `app/how-it-works/page.tsx` | Once you accept, Caraway confirms a collection window based on the vehicle, location, access, seller availability, and assigned operator. Don't move or deliver the car to an address unless that is part of the agreed pickup plan. |
| `app/how-it-works/page.tsx` | On the day, the assigned operator checks the vehicle against the description. Rules for number plates differ between transfers, cancellations, and personalised plates, so check the current TMR guidance for your situation and keep confirmation of whichever step you complete. It's also worth closing off tolls and insurance tied to the car the same day. These guides walk through each situation: | You leave the pickup with a signed receipt and the buyer's details. | `data/services.ts` | Car removal is how most of our cash for cars jobs in Brisbane end: we assess the vehicle, agree a price, then collect it. | `data/services.ts` | An owner may consider a scrap-car sale when repair costs no longer make sense, the vehicle has major mechanical or structural damage, important parts are missing, or long storage has left it deteriorated. 'Old', 'junk', and 'scrap' describe overlapping conditions rather than fixed price categories. A vehicle can still have value, but that value cannot be determined from weight or age alone. | You might sell a car for scrap when repairs no longer make sense, it has major mechanical or structural damage, important parts are missing, or long storage has left it run down. 'Old', 'junk', and 'scrap' overlap and are not fixed price categories. The car can still have value, but weight or age alone won't tell you how much. |

| `data/services.ts` | We consider the make, model, year, identity, completeness, body and drivetrain condition, location, access, and current demand for usable components or recoverable material. ## Blog guides (736)

| File(s) | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Council parking enforcement stops at your boundary. What to do about an abandoned car on private property in QLD, and why ownership decides the next step. | An abandoned car on private property in QLD is not a council parking matter. Here is what to do, and why finding the owner comes first. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | A vehicle dumped inside your gate is not a council matter, and no buyer can take it from you until the ownership question is answered. Here is the order to work through it. | A car dumped inside your gate is not a council matter. No buyer can take it until you know who owns it. Here are the steps, in order. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | A car that is not yours, sitting on land that is, is one of the few vehicle problems Brisbane City Council will not solve for you. An abandoned car on private property falls outside the council process most people reach for first, and outside what any removal operator can lawfully act on. The useful first move has nothing to do with towing. It is working out who owns the thing, and who has the authority to hand it over. | Brisbane City Council will not deal with a car that is not yours when it sits on land that is. An abandoned car on private property falls outside the council process most people try first. A removal operator cannot lawfully act on it either. The first step is not towing. It is working out who owns the car and who has the authority to hand it over. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | This catches out people who have already rung council twice. It is not indifference. Council's power to seize a vehicle comes from local laws written for public places, and once the car is past your gate those powers stop. What is left are civil questions, and civil questions run through ownership rather than enforcement. | This catches out people who have already rung council twice. Council is not ignoring you. Its power to seize a vehicle comes from local laws written for public places, and once the car is past your gate those powers stop. What is left are civil questions, and they turn on who owns the car, not on enforcement. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Start with the plate if there is one, and with the VIN stamped on the windscreen scuttle or the driver's door jamb. Photograph both, along with the car in position, the date, and anything sitting on the dash. If the vehicle looks stolen or dumped rather than merely forgotten, report it to police before you touch it — a recovered stolen car has a process of its own, and [what happens after a stolen car is recovered in QLD](/blog/stolen-car-recovered-qld) explains why being the person who scrapped it is a poor outcome. | Start with the plate if there is one, and the VIN stamped on the windscreen scuttle or the driver's door jamb. Photograph both, plus the car where it sits and anything on the dash, and note the date. If the car looks stolen or dumped, not just forgotten, report it to police before you touch it. A recovered stolen car has its own process. [What happens after a stolen car is recovered in QLD](/blog/stolen-car-recovered-qld) explains why you do not want to be the person who scrapped it. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Where the car belongs to a former tenant, a housemate who moved out, or a customer who never came back for their repair, you are dealing with someone else's goods left on your land. That is a legal question rather than a towing one. For a rental property the Residential Tenancies Authority is the place to start; otherwise a solicitor or community legal service. Get that answer before you sign anything over, because doing these steps in the wrong order turns a nuisance into a claim against you. | The car may belong to a former tenant, a housemate who moved out, or a customer who never came back for their repair. If so, you are dealing with someone else's goods left on your land. That is a legal question, not a towing one. For a rental property, start with the Residential Tenancies Authority. Otherwise, ask a solicitor or community legal service. Get that answer before you sign anything over. Doing these steps in the wrong order turns a nuisance into a claim against you. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | This is where most abandoned-vehicle enquiries end, and it is worth understanding rather than resenting. A buyer who takes a vehicle from someone with no entitlement to sell it has bought a problem, not a car, and the paperwork that follows a sale is built around the seller being able to show where that entitlement comes from. Frustration at a car you did not ask for does not create a right to dispose of it. [Selling a car that is not in your name in QLD](/blog/sell-car-not-in-my-name-qld) sets out what authority to sell actually looks like and how it is evidenced. | This is where most abandoned-vehicle enquiries end. A buyer who takes a vehicle from someone with no right to sell it has bought a problem, not a car. The paperwork after a sale relies on the seller showing where their right to sell comes from. Being fed up with a car you did not ask for does not give you a right to dispose of it. [Selling a car that is not in your name in QLD](/blog/sell-car-not-in-my-name-qld) sets out what authority to sell looks like and how to prove it. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | So the sequence matters. Identify the owner, take the legal advice that fits your situation, obtain whatever authority that process produces, and only then start talking to removal operators. Ring around for quotes at step one and you will spend a week collecting offers nobody can act on. | So the order matters. Identify the owner. Get the legal advice that fits your situation. Get whatever authority that process gives you. Only then talk to removal operators. Ring around for quotes at step one and you will spend a week collecting offers nobody can act on. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | An abandoned car on private property follows quiet frontage. Acreage blocks at Karana Downs, Pullenvale and Samford collect cars left inside an unlatched gate, sometimes a paddock's distance from anywhere a truck can reach. Industrial back lots around Rocklea, Archerfield and Wacol get them over a weekend, pushed against a fence line. Unit blocks in Annerley, Woolloongabba and Coorparoo get them in visitor bays, where the first call is to the body corporate or building manager, because the parking is common property and the rules are the scheme's own. | An abandoned car on private property turns up where the street frontage is quiet. Acreage blocks at Karana Downs, Pullenvale and Samford get cars left inside an unlatched gate, sometimes a paddock away from anywhere a truck can reach. Industrial back lots around Rocklea, Archerfield and Wacol get them over a weekend, pushed against a fence line. Unit blocks in Annerley, Woolloongabba and Coorparoo get them in visitor bays. There, call the body corporate or building manager first, because the parking is common property and the scheme sets its own rules. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Two things get worse the longer it sits. Fluids leak onto whatever surface it is parked on, and a wet summer turns a cracked sump and a gravel yard into a real mess. Access narrows too — grass grows through the wheels, the ground softens, and a spot a tilt tray could have reached in June is a bogging risk by February. Collecting it sooner is cheaper than collecting it later. | Two things get worse the longer it sits. Fluids leak onto the ground under it, and a wet summer turns a cracked sump and a gravel yard into a real mess. Access gets harder too. Grass grows through the wheels and the ground softens. A spot a tilt tray could have reached in June is a bogging risk by February. Picking it up sooner is cheaper than picking it up later. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | The far more common version of this call is the one where the answer is easy: the ute behind the shed since 2019, the second car that quietly stopped being registered, the wagon a family member left behind years ago and has since signed over. There is no ownership riddle there, and the whole thing collapses into an ordinary sale. Check what registration status the vehicle is in, gather what you have — records, keys, anything with the VIN on it — and work through [the paperwork a Queensland seller needs](/blog/what-paperwork-to-sell-a-car-qld). | More often the answer is easy. It is the ute behind the shed since 2019, the second car that quietly stopped being registered, or the wagon a family member left behind years ago and has since signed over. There is no ownership question there, so it is an ordinary sale. Check the car's registration status. Gather what you have: records, keys, anything with the VIN on it. Then work through [the paperwork a Queensland seller needs](/blog/what-paperwork-to-sell-a-car-qld). |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | If it is your vehicle and you want it assessed, call **${BUSINESS.phoneDisplay}** or request a free, no-obligation quote. [Pickup is included](/car-removal-brisbane) when Caraway buys and the vehicle and access match the supplied details, and the collection window and payment arrangement are confirmed for that job. If the ownership question is still open, sort that out first — the quote will keep. For the full picture of how Caraway prices and collects vehicles, see [cash for cars Brisbane](/). | If the car is yours, call **${BUSINESS.phoneDisplay}** or get a free, no-obligation quote. [Towing is free](/car-removal-brisbane) when we buy your car. Before pickup, we confirm the time and how you get paid. If you still do not know who owns the car, sort that out first. The quote can wait. To see how Caraway prices and picks up cars, see [cash for cars Brisbane](/). |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Not on the basis that it is inconvenient. A seller needs to be able to show an entitlement to sell, and a buyer who takes a vehicle without that has bought a problem. Identify the owner and get advice that fits your situation before arranging any disposal. | Not just because it is in your way. A seller must be able to show a right to sell, and a buyer who takes a vehicle without that has bought a problem. Identify the owner and get advice that fits your situation before you arrange any disposal. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Treat it as goods left on your land rather than as a towing job. The Residential Tenancies Authority is the starting point for a rental property, and a solicitor or community legal service for anything outside a tenancy. Do that before committing the vehicle to anyone. | Treat it as goods left on your land, not as a towing job. For a rental property, start with the Residential Tenancies Authority. For anything outside a tenancy, ask a solicitor or community legal service. Do that before you promise the car to anyone. |
| `blog/posts/abandoned-car-private-property-qld.ts` | Report it if you think the vehicle is stolen or was deliberately dumped. A recovered stolen car has its own process, and moving or disposing of one before that runs its course creates problems you do not want to own. | Report it if you think the vehicle is stolen or was deliberately dumped. A recovered stolen car has its own process. Moving or disposing of one before that process is finished creates problems for you. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | The buyer hasn't transferred the rego and the fines still have your name on them. The Queensland forms, the 14-day and 28-day clocks, and what to lodge. | The buyer hasn't transferred the rego and fines still arrive in your name. The Queensland forms, the 14-day and 28-day deadlines, and what to lodge. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | Queensland gives a buyer 14 days to lodge the transfer. Once that passes you are not stuck waiting on them: Part B, a registration check and a fine nomination are all still open. | Queensland gives a buyer 14 days to lodge the transfer. After that you do not have to wait for them. You can lodge Part B, check the registration and transfer a fine. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | You sold the car weeks ago and a toll notice has turned up with your name on it. In Queensland the registration record does not move when the money does — it moves when the transfer is lodged. If the buyer hasn't transferred the rego, the department still has you recorded as the registered operator, and everything that gets posted to that record still finds your letterbox. It is a fixable position, and there are two clocks worth understanding before you start. | You sold the car weeks ago and a toll notice has turned up with your name on it. In Queensland the registration record does not change when you are paid. It changes when the transfer is lodged. If the buyer hasn't transferred the rego, the department still has you recorded as the registered operator, and anything sent to that record still comes to your letterbox. You can fix this, and there are two deadlines to understand before you start. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | The first is fourteen days. Queensland requires the buyer to transfer the registration into their own name within 14 days of buying or receiving the vehicle, online or by lodging Part A of the Vehicle Registration Transfer Application (F3520) in person. The Vehicle Registration Regulation sets a maximum penalty for a buyer who misses that deadline without a reasonable excuse. That part is the buyer's problem — right up until the department is looking at a record that still says you. | The first is fourteen days. Queensland requires the buyer to transfer the registration into their own name within 14 days of buying or receiving the vehicle, online or by lodging Part A of the Vehicle Registration Transfer Application (F3520) in person. The Vehicle Registration Regulation sets a maximum penalty for a buyer who misses that deadline without a reasonable excuse. That part is the buyer's problem, but the record still shows your name until the transfer is lodged. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | TMR recommends that every seller complete Part B of the F3520 and have the buyer sign it on the day of sale, even when the transfer is meant to happen online. In an in-person transfer the buyer lodges Part A and the seller keeps Part B. On the day it feels like a formality. Six weeks later it is the whole argument, because it is your signed record of who took the vehicle and on what date. | TMR recommends that every seller complete Part B of the F3520 and have the buyer sign it on the day of sale, even when the transfer is meant to happen online. In an in-person transfer the buyer lodges Part A and the seller keeps Part B. On the day it feels like a formality. Six weeks later it is your proof, because it is your signed record of who took the vehicle and on what date. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | If the buyer hasn't transferred the rego within 14 days, TMR says the seller should lodge Part B in person or transfer the registration online. Neither route waits on the buyer, though the online service asks for the buyer's name exactly as TMR has it recorded and their Queensland driver licence number or customer reference number. Before you go that way, find out where the record actually sits: log in to My registrations to see whether the registration is still in your name, or message TMR or call 13 74 68. A buyer may have lodged on day thirteen without mentioning it, so rule that out first. | If the buyer hasn't transferred the rego within 14 days, TMR says the seller should lodge Part B in person or transfer the registration online. You do not need the buyer for either. The online service does ask for the buyer's name exactly as TMR has it recorded and their Queensland driver licence number or customer reference number. Check the record first: log in to My registrations to see whether the registration is still in your name, or message TMR or call 13 74 68. The buyer may have lodged on day thirteen without telling you. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | Camera-detected offences are issued to the registered operator, so a notice from Kingsford Smith Drive, the Riverside Expressway or a school zone in Carindale finds you rather than whoever was driving. Queensland's transfer-a-fine service exists for exactly this. It covers a vehicle that was sold or disposed of before the offence: you give the details of the person or organisation you sold it to and the date of sale, and you can attach supporting documents, which are required when the sale and the offence fall on the same date. The second clock is 28 days. A fine has to be transferred within 28 days of the issue date on the notice, and a fine that has already been paid cannot be transferred. | Camera-detected offences are issued to the registered operator, so a notice from Kingsford Smith Drive, the Riverside Expressway or a school zone in Carindale comes to you, not to whoever was driving. Queensland's transfer-a-fine service is made for this. It covers a vehicle that was sold or disposed of before the offence: you give the details of the person or organisation you sold it to and the date of sale, and you can attach supporting documents, which are required when the sale and the offence fall on the same date. The second deadline is 28 days. A fine has to be transferred within 28 days of the issue date on the notice, and a fine that has already been paid cannot be transferred. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | Where the online service cannot be used — the infringement cannot be found online, or you do not have the information it asks for — the same transfer is made with a statutory declaration, witnessed and posted to the Queensland Revenue Office. Standard processing time is 14 days from when the nomination is received. If the fine was issued more than 28 days ago, TMR's page sends you to the State Penalties Enforcement Registry (SPER) instead. Get the detail right rather than approximate: a false nomination is a criminal offence, which is another reason a signed Part B and a dated receipt are worth more than a thread of text messages. | Where the online service cannot be used — the infringement cannot be found online, or you do not have the information it asks for — the same transfer is made with a statutory declaration, witnessed and posted to the Queensland Revenue Office. Standard processing time is 14 days from when the nomination is received. If the fine was issued more than 28 days ago, TMR's page sends you to the State Penalties Enforcement Registry (SPER) instead. Get the details exactly right, because a false nomination is a criminal offence. That is another reason a signed Part B and a dated receipt are worth more than a thread of text messages. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | An unpaid toll notice goes to the vehicle's registered owner, and TMR's seller steps include removing any toll tag and updating your details with the toll company. If the car was on your toll account, take it off the account as its own task. [Unpaid tolls when selling a car in QLD](/blog/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld) covers who wears what once a notice has already been issued. | An unpaid toll notice goes to the vehicle's registered owner, and TMR's seller steps include removing any toll tag and updating your details with the toll company. If the car was on your toll account, take it off the account as a separate step. [Unpaid tolls when selling a car in QLD](/blog/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld) covers who pays what once a notice has been issued. |
| `blog/posts/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld.ts` | Every one of these processes asks the same question: show that the car was gone. A signed Part B, a dated receipt carrying the VIN, the odometer reading, the price and both parties' details, and photos of the vehicle leaving — the tow truck included, if it was towed — answer it in a single email instead of three phone calls and a wait. [The paperwork a Queensland seller needs](/blog/what-paperwork-to-sell-a-car-qld) sets out the full list, and every item on it is far easier to collect on the day than six weeks afterwards. | Each of these processes asks you to show the same thing: that the car was gone. A signed Part B, a dated receipt with the VIN, the odometer reading, the price and both parties' details, and photos of the vehicle leaving (including the tow truck, if it was towed) answer it in one email instead of three phone calls and a wait. [The paperwork a Queensland seller needs](/blog/what-paperwork-to-sell-a-car-qld) sets out the full list. Every item on it is far easier to collect on the day than six weeks later. |

| `blog/posts/cancel-car-insurance-after-selling-car-qld.ts` | Two insurance products come off a car when you sell it in Queensland, and only one of them handles itself. Cancel too late and you are insuring someone else's HiLux. |
| `blog/posts/cancel-car-insurance-after-selling-car-qld.ts` | Queensland CTP is compulsory third party insurance and it covers one thing — personal injury caused by the vehicle. There's nothing to cancel and nothing to claim back. | Queensland CTP is compulsory third party insurance. It covers one thing: personal injury caused by the vehicle. There's nothing to cancel and nothing to claim back. |
| `blog/posts/cancel-car-insurance-after-selling-car-qld.ts` | Your comprehensive or third party property policy works the other way around. Nobody tells your insurer the car has been sold. Direct debits keep leaving your account, renewal notices keep arriving, and the policy quietly covers a vehicle you no longer have an insurable interest in — which is another way of saying it would pay out nothing if you ever tried to claim on it. | Your comprehensive or third party property policy works the other way around. It's a contract between you and a private insurer. It covers damage to property and vehicles, not injury, and TMR cannot see it. Nobody tells your insurer the car has been sold. Direct debits keep leaving your account and renewal notices keep arriving. | `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | An approved inspection station hands back a report instead of a certificate, and a short clock starts running. | `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | A failed roadworthy in QLD is not a verdict on the car. It is a list. The approved inspection station keeps the certificate and hands back an inspection report setting out which items did not meet the standard — a tyre worn past its indicators, a park brake that will not hold on a slope, corrosion in a structural area. What happens over the next fortnight decides whether this costs you a modest repair bill or a second full inspection. | A failed roadworthy in QLD is not a verdict on the car. It is a list. The approved inspection station keeps the certificate and gives you an inspection report. It lists the items that did not meet the standard, such as a tyre worn past its indicators, a park brake that will not hold on a slope, or corrosion in a structural area. What you do in the next fortnight decides whether this costs you a modest repair bill or a second full inspection. |
| `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | The two documents get confused constantly, and they do different things. An inspection report records that a vehicle presented for a safety certificate did not meet the standard on the day. A defect notice is a separate instrument that restricts how a vehicle may be used on the road until the work is cleared and the notice is lifted; our guide to [defect notices in Queensland](/blog/car-defect-notice-qld) sets out that process. Failing an inspection does not, by itself, cancel your registration either. Keep the report: it itemises what the examiner found, it is what a workshop quotes against, and the reinspection arrangement depends on it. Photograph it before it disappears into the glovebox for a fortnight. | People often confuse the two documents, and they do different things. An inspection report records that a vehicle presented for a safety certificate did not meet the standard on the day. A defect notice is a separate instrument that restricts how a vehicle may be used on the road until the work is cleared and the notice is lifted. Our guide to [defect notices in Queensland](/blog/car-defect-notice-qld) sets out that process. Failing an inspection does not, by itself, cancel your registration either. Keep the report. It lists what the examiner found, a workshop quotes against it, and the reinspection depends on it. Take a photo of it before it gets lost in the glovebox. |
| `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | Transport and Main Roads sets the reinspection rule through the approved inspection station scheme: where a vehicle that failed its first inspection is brought back to the station that issued the report within 14 days, that station must arrange the reinspection, normally with the same approved examiner. Let the fortnight lapse, or take the car somewhere else, and you are back to a full inspection from the beginning. | Transport and Main Roads sets the reinspection rule through the approved inspection station scheme: where a vehicle that failed its first inspection is brought back to the station that issued the report within 14 days, that station must arrange the reinspection, normally with the same approved examiner. If the 14 days run out, or you take the car somewhere else, you start again with a full inspection. |
| `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | How much the reinspection costs is a separate question, and it varies. Some stations build one reinspection into the original booking; a mobile examiner who has to drive back out to Capalaba or Springwood may charge a call-out for the return trip. Ask when you book the repair, not when you arrive. | The cost of the reinspection varies. Some stations include one reinspection in the original booking. | `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | Fourteen days is tighter than it sounds if a part has to come from interstate. | `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | Three findings turn a failed roadworthy into a decision about the car rather than a repair job. Structural corrosion is the big one in Brisbane: bayside and northern suburbs from Wynnum to Redcliffe get salt air on top of subtropical humidity, and rust that has reached a chassis rail or a suspension mounting point is a fabrication job rather than a repair. Steering and suspension wear across several components is the second, because the parts are rarely worn one at a time. The third is anything electrical behind the dash — an SRS warning that will not clear can burn diagnostic hours before a single part is fitted. | Three findings turn a failed roadworthy into a decision about the car, not just a repair job. Structural corrosion is the big one in Brisbane. Bayside and northern suburbs from Wynnum to Redcliffe get salt air on top of subtropical humidity, and rust that has reached a chassis rail or a suspension mounting point is a fabrication job, not a simple repair. The second is steering and suspension wear across several components, because the parts rarely wear one at a time. The third is anything electrical behind the dash. An SRS warning that will not clear can take hours to diagnose before a single part is fitted. |
| `blog/posts/car-failed-roadworthy-qld.ts` | For a car with high kilometres and a modest resale value, any of the three usually produces a quote above what the car would fetch. | `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | Storage accrues by the day while an impounded vehicle sits in the holding yard, and the deadline that costs owners the car altogether lands after the impoundment ends rather than during it. | Storage is charged by the day while an impounded car sits in the holding yard. | `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | A car impounded in QLD keeps costing money while it sits. The tow is charged once, storage is charged by the day, and both fall on the owner rather than on whoever was driving when police stepped in. Queensland Police Service says a vehicle will not be released at the end of an impoundment period, or after a successful early release application, until the fees owing to the tow provider are paid. Knowing what that clock looks like from day one is what stops a short matter turning into a lost car. | A car impounded in QLD keeps costing money while it sits. Queensland Police Service says a vehicle will not be released at the end of an impoundment period, or after a successful early release application, until the fees owing to the tow provider are paid. Know the deadlines from day one so a short matter does not end in a lost car. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | QPS draws a line between the two. Impoundment means the vehicle is towed to a holding yard. Immobilisation means the number plates are confiscated and the vehicle is kept somewhere other than a holding yard. Either can follow a driver being charged with a type 1 or type 2 vehicle-related offence under the Police Powers and Responsibilities Act 2000, and the periods are set by that Act rather than negotiated at a counter — commonly seven or 90 days, depending on the offence type and what already sits on the driver's record. | QPS treats the two differently. Impoundment means the vehicle is towed to a holding yard. Immobilisation means the number plates are confiscated and the vehicle is kept somewhere other than a holding yard. Either can follow a driver being charged with a type 1 or type 2 vehicle-related offence under the Police Powers and Responsibilities Act 2000. The periods are set by that Act and cannot be negotiated at a counter. They are commonly seven or 90 days, depending on the offence type and what is already on the driver's record. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | The driver and the registered owner are often not the same person, which is where this becomes expensive for someone who was nowhere near the car. A hatchback lent to an adult child for a weekend goes to the yard on the same terms as one the owner was driving, and the storage bill accrues against the vehicle either way. | The driver and the registered owner are often different people, so the bill can land on someone who was nowhere near the car. A hatchback lent to an adult child for a weekend goes to the yard on the same terms as one the owner was driving, and storage is charged against the vehicle either way. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | Towing and storage are the owner's to settle, and QPS is direct about the consequence: the car does not leave the yard until the tow provider's fees are paid. Storage is the part that compounds. A seven-day matter is a short bill; a 90-day impoundment is three months of daily charges on a vehicle nobody is driving, and on an older car the total can overtake what the thing is worth before the period is even up. | The owner pays the towing and storage, and QPS is clear about what happens otherwise: the car does not leave the yard until the tow provider's fees are paid. Storage is the part that adds up. A seven-day matter is a short bill. A 90-day impoundment is three months of daily charges on a car nobody is driving, and on an older car the total can pass what the car is worth before the period ends. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | Two other things keep running in the background. Registration expires on its ordinary date whether the car is in a yard or a driveway, so a vehicle that goes in with two months of rego left can come out unregistered — and it then cannot simply be driven home. Moving it means the applicable permit and CTP cover, or a tow, which our guide to [parking an unregistered car in QLD](/blog/park-unregistered-car-street-qld) sets out in detail. The second is mechanical: three months of standing usually means a flat battery, soft tyres and stale fuel, so budget for [what a tow costs in Brisbane](/blog/tow-truck-cost-brisbane) rather than assuming you will drive it out. | Two other things keep running. Registration expires on its usual date whether the car is in a yard or a driveway, so a vehicle that goes in with two months of rego left can come out unregistered. Moving it means the applicable permit and CTP cover, or a tow, which our guide to [parking an unregistered car in QLD](/blog/park-unregistered-car-street-qld) explains. The second is mechanical. Three months of standing usually means a flat battery, soft tyres and stale fuel, so budget for [what a tow costs in Brisbane](/blog/tow-truck-cost-brisbane) and do not assume you will drive it out. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | An application for early release goes to the Commissioner of Police. QPS lists the grounds it will consider: that not having the vehicle is causing severe financial or physical hardship; that the cause of the offence has been corrected, such as a modification brought back into compliance; that you are the owner and the offence happened without your consent; or that the impoundment or immobilisation was unreasonable. QPS says a decision should follow within five working days once all documentation has been received, which makes the supporting documents the part worth getting right the first time. | An application for early release goes to the Commissioner of Police. QPS lists the grounds it will consider: that not having the vehicle is causing severe financial or physical hardship; that the cause of the offence has been corrected, such as a modification brought back into compliance; that you are the owner and the offence happened without your consent; or that the impoundment or immobilisation was unreasonable. QPS says a decision should follow within five working days once all documentation has been received, so get the supporting documents right the first time. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | Where charges are withdrawn or the driver is found not guilty, QPS says the tow and storage fees are payable by the Queensland Police Service under section 111 of the Act rather than by the owner. That is worth knowing before anyone pays a yard invoice simply to make the problem go away. | Where charges are withdrawn or the driver is found not guilty, QPS says the tow and storage fees are payable by the Queensland Police Service under section 111 of the Act rather than by the owner. Know this before you pay a yard invoice just to make the problem go away. |
| `blog/posts/car-impounded-qld.ts` | The deadline that actually costs people their vehicle arrives after the impoundment period, not during it. QPS says that if the owner does not collect within 30 days after the period ends, the vehicle is taken to have been forfeited to the State, and the Commissioner may then sell it by public auction or dispose of it in the way the Commissioner considers appropriate. Proposed disposals are published by QPS. Leaving the car in the yard because the fees have become unaffordable is a decision with an end date attached, not a delay. | The deadline that costs people their vehicle comes after the impoundment period, not during it. | `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | Two names on the rego means two people with a stake in the sale, whatever the transfer form asks for. Here is how Queensland handles the transfer, and what to settle first when the other operator is unwilling, unavailable or gone. | Two names on the rego means two people with a stake in the sale. Here is how Queensland handles the transfer, and what to settle first if the other person will not agree, cannot be reached or has died. |
| `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | Two names on the registration usually means two people with a say in the sale. A car registered in two names in Queensland still goes through the ordinary seller and buyer transfer steps, but Transport and Main Roads (TMR) says a registration certificate does not prove ownership, so being named on it, or holding the keys, does not settle who is entitled to sell. That is a formality most of the time. It stops being one the moment the other name has moved interstate, stopped replying, or died. | Two names on the registration usually means two people with a say in the sale. A car registered in two names in Queensland still goes through the ordinary seller and buyer transfer steps. But Transport and Main Roads (TMR) says a registration certificate does not prove ownership, so being named on it, or holding the keys, does not settle who is entitled to sell. Most of the time that is a formality. It stops being one when the other person has moved interstate, stopped replying, or died. |
| `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | The clock is fourteen days. The buyer must transfer the registration into their name within fourteen days of buying or receiving the vehicle, and the buyer carries the cost: the transfer fee and vehicle registration duty are theirs, and a seller pays nothing to have the car leave their name. If the buyer does not finish within that time, TMR says the seller should lodge Part B in person or transfer the registration online, which matters more than usual here — until the transfer goes through, both of you are still on the record. Our [guide to transferring car ownership in QLD](/blog/how-to-transfer-car-ownership-qld) walks the whole sequence, and [what to do when a buyer hasn't transferred the rego](/blog/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld) covers the follow-up. | The deadline is fourteen days. The buyer must transfer the registration into their name within fourteen days of buying or receiving the vehicle, and the buyer carries the cost: the transfer fee and vehicle registration duty are theirs, and a seller pays nothing to have the car leave their name. If the buyer does not finish within that time, TMR says the seller should lodge Part B in person or transfer the registration online. That matters more than usual here, because until the transfer goes through, both of you are still on the record. Our [guide to transferring car ownership in QLD](/blog/how-to-transfer-car-ownership-qld) walks through the whole sequence, and [what to do when a buyer hasn't transferred the rego](/blog/buyer-not-transferred-rego-qld) covers the follow-up. |
| `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | The safety certificate rule does not soften because there are two names on the registration. Selling a registered vehicle to anyone other than a licensed motor dealer means giving the buyer a current safety certificate, valid for a private seller for two months or 2,000 kilometres from issue, whichever runs out first, and a single certificate covers one transfer only. [Queensland safety certificates](/blog/how-to-get-a-roadworthy-certificate-brisbane) covers where to book one and what an inspector is working to. | The safety certificate rule is the same when there are two names on the registration. Selling a registered vehicle to anyone other than a licensed motor dealer means giving the buyer a current safety certificate, valid for a private seller for two months or 2,000 kilometres from issue, whichever runs out first, and a single certificate covers one transfer only. [Queensland safety certificates](/blog/how-to-get-a-roadworthy-certificate-brisbane) covers where to book one and what an inspector checks. |
| `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | A registration counter is not where this gets settled. TMR's transfer pages cover how a registration moves from a seller to a buyer; they say nothing about who paid for the car, who drove it, or who is being unreasonable, and a registration certificate does not prove ownership. Whatever the form asks for, a sale the other person has not agreed to is a disagreement between the two of you, and it belongs in a written agreement or in front of a solicitor rather than at the registration desk. | This does not get settled at a registration counter. TMR's transfer pages cover how a registration moves from a seller to a buyer. They say nothing about who paid for the car, who drove it, or who is being unreasonable, and a registration certificate does not prove ownership. Whatever the form asks for, a sale the other person has not agreed to is a disagreement between the two of you. Settle it in a written agreement or with a solicitor, not at the registration desk. |
| `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | What usually shifts it is money or paper. One of you buys the other out and the car goes into a single name first, or the split of the sale proceeds gets agreed in writing before anyone signs anything. Where the other person is willing but genuinely unavailable — working away, overseas, in hospital — a formal authority can carry their side, and [selling a car under power of attorney in QLD](/blog/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld) sets out what that instrument has to say before it will be accepted. A text message saying to go ahead and sell it is not one. | What usually fixes it is money or paperwork. One of you buys the other out and the car goes into a single name first. If the other person is willing but cannot be there (working away, overseas, in hospital), a formal authority can cover their side. A text message saying to go ahead and sell it is not one. |
| `blog/posts/car-registered-two-names-qld.ts` | This is not the ordinary sale transfer, and treating it as one wastes a trip. TMR has a separate pathway for a deceased registered operator. Work that out before you accept an offer on the car in a parent's driveway at Kenmore or Redcliffe, not after. | This is not the ordinary sale transfer, and treating it as one wastes a trip. | `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | The tow truck takes the car, and everything stored in it goes along for the ride. Here's what a modern vehicle quietly remembers about its owner, and the ten minutes it takes to clear it out. | When the car goes, everything stored in it goes too. Here is what a modern car remembers about its owner, and how to clear it out in ten minutes. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | A car leaves the driveway carrying more of you than most people expect. The sat nav knows your street. The infotainment system holds your phone book and every call you took on the way to work. The mirror or the visor may have your garage door programmed into it, and your licence plate is probably still sitting on a toll account. Ten minutes to delete personal data from your car is the difference between a clean sale and a stranger holding your address, your contacts and a working remote for your garage. | A car holds more about you than most people expect. The sat nav knows your street. The infotainment system holds your phone book and your call history. The mirror or the visor may have your garage door programmed into it, and your licence plate is probably still on a toll account. Take ten minutes to delete personal data from your car before you sell it. Otherwise a stranger could end up with your address, your contacts and a working remote for your garage. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | Pairing a phone does more than pipe music through the speakers. Most head units copy across contacts, call history and often text messages, and they keep that copy on the car's own storage — deleting the car from your phone's Bluetooth list does nothing to the version sitting in the dash. A 2013 Corolla with a basic Bluetooth radio still has a paired-devices list with your name on it. | Pairing a phone does more than play music through the speakers. Most head units copy your contacts, call history and often text messages, and keep that copy on the car's own storage. Deleting the car from your phone's Bluetooth list does nothing to the copy in the dash. A 2013 Corolla with a basic Bluetooth radio still has a paired-devices list with your name on it. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | The one people miss is the garage. Plenty of cars have a built-in opener — three buttons in the rear-view mirror or the overhead console — paired to the roller door. Sell the car with your address in the sat nav and the door still paired in the mirror, and you have handed over both halves of the problem at once. | The one people miss is the garage. Plenty of cars have a built-in opener — three buttons in the rear-view mirror or the overhead console — paired to the roller door. Sell the car with your address in the sat nav and the door still paired in the mirror, and the buyer has both your address and a way in. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | Start with a factory reset of the infotainment system, which is usually buried under Settings, then General or System, then something along the lines of "Factory reset" or "Delete personal data". That one action typically clears paired devices, navigation history and profiles together. If your car has no master reset, work through the lists by hand: delete each paired phone, clear recent destinations, and remove the HOME and saved favourites entries rather than just editing them. | Start with a factory reset of the infotainment system. It is usually under Settings, then General or System, then an option such as "Factory reset" or "Delete personal data". That one action typically clears paired devices, navigation history and profiles together. If your car has no master reset, work through the lists by hand: delete each paired phone, clear recent destinations, and remove the HOME and saved favourites entries rather than just editing them. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | Then sweep the physical storage, because plenty of data never touches the touchscreen. Pull the SD card out of the dashcam and check whether the camera is hardwired to the fusebox. Retrieve USB sticks from the console, any SD card in the nav slot, and the aftermarket GPS tracker if an old finance deal left one behind. Registration papers and service invoices in the glovebox carry your name and address in plain text. Our [pickup preparation checklist](/blog/preparing-your-car-for-pickup) covers the rest of that sweep. Last, change the code at the garage motor rather than only unpairing the car — re-coding the opener is the fix that holds regardless of what is still stored in a mirror you no longer control. | Then check the physical storage, because plenty of data never touches the touchscreen. Pull the SD card out of the dashcam and check whether the camera is hardwired to the fusebox. Take USB sticks from the console, any SD card in the nav slot, and the aftermarket GPS tracker if an old finance deal left one behind. Registration papers and service invoices in the glovebox show your name and address. Our [pickup preparation checklist](/blog/preparing-your-car-for-pickup) covers the rest. Last, change the code at the garage motor rather than only unpairing the car. Re-coding the opener works whatever is still stored in a mirror you no longer control. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | You can delete personal data from your car in ten minutes. The accounts indexed by its registration number take a little longer. Our guide to [unpaid tolls when selling a car in QLD](/blog/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld) sets out the order to work through. | You can delete personal data from your car in ten minutes. | `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | Council parking is the quiet one. Brisbane City Council's regulated parking permits for residents are linked to a vehicle's registration rather than displayed as a windscreen sticker, so a permit still tied to a plate you no longer own is worth sorting out with council when you change vehicles. The same goes for workplace, hospital or airport permits registered against a plate. Insurance is the other loose end — our guide to [cancelling car insurance after a sale](/blog/cancel-car-insurance-after-selling-car-qld) explains why cancelling too early can cost you cover you still need — and if your roadside assistance is attached to the vehicle rather than to you personally, ring RACQ or your club and ask which one you hold. | Council parking is the quiet one. Brisbane City Council's regulated parking permits for residents are linked to a vehicle's registration rather than displayed as a windscreen sticker, so a permit still tied to a plate you no longer own is worth sorting out with council when you change vehicles. The same goes for workplace, hospital or airport permits registered against a plate. Insurance is the other loose end. If your roadside assistance is attached to the vehicle rather than to you personally, ring RACQ or your club and ask which one you hold. |
| `blog/posts/delete-personal-data-from-car-before-selling.ts` | Scrap and non-running cars come with a wrinkle: a dead battery means no touchscreen, and no touchscreen means no factory reset. A jump pack for a few minutes is usually enough to bring the head unit up long enough to wipe it. If that isn't practical — a flooded car, a burnt-out loom, a vehicle that hasn't turned over since 2019 — take the SD cards and papers and re-code the garage opener instead. Infotainment modules do get pulled and resold out of wrecked cars, so run the reset whenever the car will still power up. | Scrap and non-running cars have one extra problem: a dead battery means no touchscreen, and no touchscreen means no factory reset. | `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | Queensland gives a lapsed registration three months before it is gone for good, and the gap between reinstating inside that window and registering from scratch is measured in inspections, repairs and back-paid fees. | Queensland gives a lapsed registration three months before it is gone for good. Reinstating inside that window means back-paid fees. Registering from scratch after it means an inspection and often repairs. |
| `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | Registration lapses quietly in Queensland. There is no windscreen label to glance at and no annual inspection forcing the issue, so expired rego in QLD is usually noticed weeks after the fact — by a reminder in the letterbox, or by someone running the plate before a sale. From the due date itself the vehicle cannot lawfully be driven on a road, and a deadline starts running that decides whether you renew the registration you had or apply for a new one. | Registration lapses quietly in Queensland. There is no windscreen label to check and no annual inspection to prompt you, so expired rego in QLD is usually noticed weeks later, from a reminder in the letterbox or when someone runs the plate before a sale. From the due date the vehicle cannot lawfully be driven on a road. A deadline also starts that decides whether you renew the registration you had or apply for a new one. |
| `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | That status has teeth close to home. Brisbane City Council's Health, Safety and Amenity Local Law 2021 makes it an offence to park an unregistered vehicle on a road, and Council treats one left on a road or road-related area as unmanaged. The kerb outside a Wynnum or Annerley house is not somewhere to leave the car while you think about it. Inside the property boundary is a different question, and our guide to [parking an unregistered car in QLD](/blog/park-unregistered-car-street-qld) works through both. | That matters close to home. Brisbane City Council's Health, Safety and Amenity Local Law 2021 makes it an offence to park an unregistered vehicle on a road, and Council treats one left on a road or road-related area as unmanaged. The kerb outside a Wynnum or Annerley house is not somewhere to leave the car while you think about it. Inside the property boundary is a different question, and our guide to [parking an unregistered car in QLD](/blog/park-unregistered-car-street-qld) works through both. |
| `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | The practical effect is that waiting costs money without buying anything. Three months of unused registration plus the late fee is the price of a decision deferred, which is worth knowing before the reminder gets filed under later. | In practice, waiting costs money and buys nothing. Put the decision off for three months and you pay for three months of unused registration plus the late fee. |
| `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | Past that three-month mark the old registration is gone and the vehicle has to go through registration as though it were new. That is where the cost changes shape rather than just growing. A new registration needs a Queensland safety certificate from an approved inspection station, and a car that has been standing since autumn tends to need work to earn one — perished tyres, seized brakes, a flat battery, whatever the last service was putting off. Our [Queensland safety certificate guide](/blog/how-to-get-a-roadworthy-certificate-brisbane) covers what an inspector looks at. | Past that three-month mark the old registration is gone and the vehicle has to go through registration as though it were new. Our [Queensland safety certificate guide](/blog/how-to-get-a-roadworthy-certificate-brisbane) covers what an inspector looks at. |
| `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | Getting the car to that inspection is its own step. TMR allows some limited registration-related journeys without a permit and requires an unregistered vehicle permit for others, with conditions attached to both. A vehicle that fails the relevant inspection cannot then be driven or towed on its own wheels and has to be transported, so the tow is a real line in the budget rather than a footnote. | Getting the car to that inspection is its own step. TMR allows some limited registration-related journeys without a permit and requires an unregistered vehicle permit for others, with conditions attached to both. A vehicle that fails the relevant inspection cannot then be driven or towed on its own wheels and has to be transported, so budget for the tow. |
| `blog/posts/expired-rego-qld.ts` | Do the arithmetic in one sitting. Inside three months it is back-paid registration plus the late fee. Put that against what the vehicle is genuinely worth in its current state — the same comparison our [repair or sell decision guide](/blog/repair-or-sell-your-car-brisbane) sets out for cars whose registration never lapsed. | Add it all up at once. | `blog/posts/gifting-car-family-member-qld.ts` | Queensland's duty exemption for a family car gift covers a shorter list of relatives than most people assume, and only one relationship gets out of the safety certificate. Here is who qualifies for each. |
| `blog/posts/gifting-car-family-member-qld.ts` | Gifting a car to a family member in Queensland costs less than selling one to them, provided both the relationship and the price qualify. The state waives vehicle registration duty on a genuine family gift, defines family more narrowly than most people expect, and still wants a safety certificate for nearly all of these transfers. Sorting those three points out before anyone signs a transfer form is what keeps the handover from stalling at a customer service centre. | Gifting a car to a family member in Queensland costs less than selling one to them, provided both the relationship and the price qualify. The state waives vehicle registration duty on a genuine family gift, defines family more narrowly than most people expect, and still wants a safety certificate for nearly all of these transfers. Sort out those three points before anyone signs a transfer form, so the handover does not stall at a customer service centre. |
| `blog/posts/gifting-car-family-member-qld.ts` | The second test is money. A gift means the vehicle is not sold for any amount, so a token dollar on the receipt turns the transaction back into a sale. Understating the price does not help either — where what is paid falls short of what the vehicle is worth, duty is assessed on the value of the vehicle rather than the figure written on the paperwork. | The second test is money. Where what is paid falls short of what the vehicle is worth, duty is assessed on the value of the vehicle rather than the figure written on the paperwork. |
| `blog/posts/gifting-car-family-member-qld.ts` | The exemption for gifting a car to a family member is claimed as part of the transfer rather than afterwards, online or in person. Duty is payable by the person taking on the registration, so the exemption matters most to whoever is receiving the car. Where duty has already been paid on a transfer that should have been exempt, a written request for a reassessment with a completed transfer of vehicle registration statutory declaration is the route to a refund. The rest of it is an ordinary change of registered operator — our [guide to transferring car ownership in Queensland](/blog/how-to-transfer-car-ownership-qld) sets out the forms, the fees and the order they happen in. | The exemption for gifting a car to a family member is claimed as part of the transfer rather than afterwards, online or in person. | `blog/posts/gifting-car-family-member-qld.ts` | Parent to child is the transfer this catches most often, and it is also the transfer most likely to involve an old car. Perished rubber and corroded brake lines are ordinary findings on a vehicle that has done more standing than driving, and neither is a cheap line on an inspection report. | Parent to child is the transfer this catches most often, and it is also the one most likely to involve an old car. | `blog/posts/gifting-car-family-member-qld.ts` | Gifting a car to a family member only makes sense while the car is worth the work. | `blog/posts/how-car-recycling-works-australia.ts` | An end-of-life vehicle may be reused whole, dismantled for suitable components, processed for recoverable materials, or sent through a combination of those pathways. The actual outcome depends on the vehicle, its legal status, the receiving operator, and the downstream facilities involved. | An end-of-life vehicle may be reused whole, dismantled for suitable parts, processed for recoverable materials, or a mix of these. | `blog/posts/how-car-recycling-works-australia.ts` | Before collection, a buyer should identify the vehicle, confirm the seller's authority, record its condition and completeness, and agree on the payment, pickup, and receipt terms. | `blog/posts/how-car-recycling-works-australia.ts` | A dismantler may assess engines, transmissions, body panels, lights, wheels, electronics, interior items, and other components for reuse. | `blog/posts/how-car-recycling-works-australia.ts` | After appropriate component removal and depollution, a remaining body may be baled, crushed, or shredded. | `blog/posts/how-to-avoid-cash-for-cars-scams-brisbane.ts` | Another is the towing-fee sting. | `blog/posts/how-to-avoid-cash-for-cars-scams-brisbane.ts` | How you get paid is where most real losses happen, so this is worth getting right. Be cautious of anyone who wants to take the car now and "transfer the money later", who offers a cheque from an unfamiliar business, or who sends a bank-transfer screenshot as "proof" while the funds never actually land. Screenshots are trivial to fake, and a pending transfer can be cancelled. The safe rule is simple: the car does not leave your property until the payment has genuinely cleared. With cash, count it before the vehicle is loaded. With a bank transfer, confirm the money has actually appeared in your account — not just that a receipt was shown to you — before you hand over the keys. | Payment is where most real losses happen. Be wary of anyone who wants to take the car now and "transfer the money later", who offers a cheque from a business you do not know, or who sends a bank-transfer screenshot as "proof" when the money never arrives. Screenshots are easy to fake, and a pending transfer can be cancelled. The safe rule: the car does not leave your property until the payment has cleared. With cash, count it before the car is loaded. With a bank transfer, check the money is in your account before you hand over the keys. Being shown a receipt is not enough. |
| `blog/posts/how-to-avoid-cash-for-cars-scams-brisbane.ts` | Treat an unexpected overpayment followed by a request to send the difference elsewhere as a serious warning sign. Contact your bank through its official channel and resolve the original transaction before sending money or releasing the vehicle. | An unexpected overpayment followed by a request to send the difference somewhere else is a serious warning sign. Do not send a refund based only on a message, a screenshot or a payment that appears to be on its way. | `blog/posts/how-to-cancel-car-rego-qld.ts` | To cancel rego in QLD you have two main pathways. The first is online through the TMR website if you have a linked QGov account. | `blog/posts/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld.ts` | A financed-vehicle sale needs the lender's current payout instructions, a PPSR check, a documented settlement flow, and evidence that any security interest is discharged. | To sell a car with finance owing, you need the lender's current payout instructions, a PPSR check, a written settlement plan, and proof that any security interest is discharged. |
| `blog/posts/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld.ts` | A vehicle can be connected to a loan or another security interest recorded on the Personal Property Securities Register (PPSR). The finance contract and the lender's instructions decide whether and how it may be sold before the debt is cleared. Start with the lender and put the settlement steps in writing. Do not hand over the vehicle on the assumption that a registration transfer alone removes a security interest. |
| `blog/posts/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld.ts` | Do not assume you can transfer a financed vehicle without the lender's agreement. | `blog/posts/lost-rego-papers-qld.ts` | A registration certificate names the registered operator and describes the vehicle. That is a different thing from proving who owns it. A buyer who asks for photo identification matching the registration record is not being difficult — they are doing the only check available to them. | A registration certificate names the registered operator and describes the vehicle. | `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | When a mechanic won't release your car in Queensland, the lien behind it has real limits — including on storage fees. | `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | A repairer's lien is narrower than most people assume: it secures the work you authorised, it is not a power of sale, and Queensland's storage lien law leaves motor vehicles out of it entirely. | A repairer's lien is narrower than most people think. It secures the work you authorised. It is not a power of sale. And Queensland's storage lien law does not cover motor vehicles. |
| `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | A Queensland repairer can lawfully keep your car until you pay for the work you authorised. The right is called a repairer's lien and it is older than most of the workshops using it. What surprises people is how narrow it is: when a mechanic won't release your car, the lien covers the repair debt on that vehicle and not much else — not a rolling storage charge, and not a right to sell the car out from under you. | A Queensland repairer can lawfully keep your car until you pay for the work you authorised. The right is called a repairer's lien and it is older than most of the workshops using it. What surprises people is how narrow it is. When a mechanic won't release your car, the lien covers the repair debt on that vehicle and not much else. It does not cover a rolling storage charge, and it is not a right to sell the car. |
| `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | The lien is possessory. It exists because the repairer has the car, it lasts only while the repairer keeps holding it, and it secures payment for work done on that vehicle with the owner's authority. Hand the keys back and it is generally gone. That is why a workshop which lets you take a car home on a promise to pay rarely lets you take it a second time. | The lien is possessory. It exists because the repairer has the car, it lasts only while the repairer keeps holding it, and it secures payment for work done on that vehicle with the owner's authority. Hand the keys back and it is generally gone. That is why a workshop that lets you take a car home on a promise to pay rarely lets you take it a second time. |
| `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | Work you authorised is the operative phrase. Queensland Fair Trading's guidance on getting a car repaired is blunt about the paperwork — get a written quote before the job starts, and authorise the repairs that get done. Approval for a timing belt does not quietly become authority for a head gasket, and a bill that grew without a phone call is the part to query first. | "Work you authorised" is the key phrase. Queensland Fair Trading's guidance on getting a car repaired is clear about the paperwork: get a written quote before the job starts, and authorise the repairs that get done. Approval for a timing belt is not approval for a head gasket. If the bill grew without a phone call, query that part first. |
| `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | The second limit matters more once the numbers turn ugly. A common law lien is a right to retain the car, not a power of sale. A repairer who wants to turn your vehicle into money generally needs a court order or your written consent. A letter threatening to sell the car in fourteen days is not, by itself, the legal position, and it is worth answering in writing rather than ignoring. | The second limit matters more when the bill gets large. A common law lien is a right to retain the car, not a power of sale. A repairer who wants to sell your vehicle to recover the money generally needs a court order or your written consent. A letter threatening to sell the car in fourteen days is not, by itself, the legal position. Answer it in writing rather than ignoring it. |
| `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | Workshops on the industrial strips around Rocklea, Acacia Ridge and Coopers Plains are genuinely short of space, and a car parked out the back for three months costs them something real. | `blog/posts/mechanic-wont-release-car-qld.ts` | Queensland's Storage Liens Act 1973 is not the fallback either, which catches out both sides of these arguments. The Act gives a storer a lien over goods left with them and, after notice, a power of sale — but its definition of goods leaves motor vehicles out. The statutory storage-lien machinery people find online does not reach a car sitting behind a Brisbane workshop. The storage-lien process people find online does not apply to a car sitting behind a Brisbane workshop. If storage was never quoted, never mentioned and never agreed, say so in writing early, before the amount grows. |

| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | QLD gives private car sales no cooling-off period and no statutory warranty, yet three guarantees still bind the seller.
