| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | A demand for money back is not the same as a right to it. Here is what a Queensland private seller is still on the hook for once the keys have changed hands, and what a buyer cannot reach. | A buyer who demands their money back does not always have a right to it. Here is what a Queensland private seller is still responsible for after the sale, and what a buyer cannot claim. |
| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | The message usually turns up on day four. The car is making a noise, the buyer is unhappy, and they want their money back. A private car sale refund is not something a Queensland buyer can simply demand: the protections that oblige a seller to take a vehicle back attach to licensed dealers, not to someone selling one car out of their own driveway at Wynnum. What does survive a private sale is narrower than most people on either side of the handover expect. | The message usually turns up on day four. The car is making a noise, the buyer is unhappy, and they want their money back. A Queensland buyer cannot simply demand a private car sale refund. The protections that oblige a seller to take a vehicle back apply to licensed dealers, not to someone selling one car from their own driveway at Wynnum. What still applies after a private sale is narrower than most buyers and sellers expect. |
| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | Two of the protections buyers assume they have sit entirely on the dealer side of the market. A used vehicle bought from a licensed motor dealer carries a cooling-off period of one business day, ending at 5pm on the next business day, and a statutory warranty at no extra cost. A car bought at auction or on consignment gets no cooling-off period, although an auctioned vehicle still carries the statutory warranty unless it is one of the exempt types. That warranty runs three months or 5,000km for a vehicle under 160,000km and built within the previous ten years, and one month or 1,000km for anything older or higher. Buy the same car from a stranger on Marketplace and neither one exists. | Two protections buyers assume they have exist only on the dealer side of the market. A used vehicle bought from a licensed motor dealer carries a cooling-off period of one business day, ending at 5pm on the next business day, and a statutory warranty at no extra cost. A car bought at auction or on consignment gets no cooling-off period, although an auctioned vehicle still carries the statutory warranty unless it is one of the exempt types. That warranty runs three months or 5,000km for a vehicle under 160,000km and built within the previous ten years, and one month or 1,000km for anything older or higher. Buy the same car from a stranger on Marketplace and neither one exists. |
| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | That is not a loophole anyone is exploiting. It is the line the Queensland scheme deliberately draws between a business that sells cars and a person who is selling one. The price gap between the two usually reflects exactly that difference. | That is not a loophole. It is the line the Queensland scheme draws on purpose between a business that sells cars and a person selling one. The price gap between the two usually reflects that difference. |
| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | Those three do more work than they sound like, because they are the finance ones. If money was still owing and the security interest was never discharged, the buyer's complaint is no longer about a gearbox — it is that the car was never entirely yours to hand over. That is why [selling a car with finance owing](/blog/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld) has to be settled before the keys move rather than after. A buyer who runs a PPSR search the week after the sale and finds a registered interest has something real, and no amount of "sold as is" wording in a listing answers it. | Those three matter more than they sound, because they are the ones about finance. If money was still owing and the security interest was never discharged, the buyer's complaint is no longer about a gearbox. It is that the car was never fully yours to hand over. That is why [selling a car with finance owing](/blog/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld) has to be settled before you hand over the keys, not after. A buyer who runs a PPSR search the week after the sale and finds a registered interest has a real complaint, and "sold as is" wording in a listing does not answer it. |
| `blog/posts/private-car-sale-refund-qld.ts` | QCAT is the tribunal Queenslanders reach for first, and on this it is the wrong door. Its motor vehicle dispute jurisdiction is built around a purchase from a licensed motor dealer, and QCAT says an application about a private sale of a motor vehicle will be dismissed. A buyer determined to press the point is left with an ordinary civil claim, which for amounts up to $150,000 is started in the Magistrates Court — a filing fee, a hearing date, and the burden of proof sitting with them. | QCAT is the tribunal Queenslanders think of first, and for this it is the wrong place. Its motor vehicle dispute jurisdiction is built around a purchase from a licensed motor dealer, and QCAT says an application about a private sale of a motor vehicle will be dismissed. A buyer who wants to press the point is left with an ordinary civil claim, which for amounts up to $150,000 is started in the Magistrates Court. That means a filing fee, a hearing date, and the burden of proof sitting with them. |

| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | The Queensland rego record says you, the lease says the financier, and a deed of novation sits in between. Here is what has to be settled before an offer on a salary-packaged car means anything. | The Queensland rego is in your name, the financier holds title, and a deed of novation sits in between. Here is what to settle before you take offers on a salary-packaged car. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | You are on the rego, you hold the keys, and you still cannot sell the car. That is what most people find the first time they try to get out of a novated lease early — the finance company holds title under the lease, and the arrangement runs between you, your employer and that financier rather than sitting with you alone. The registration certificate is the one document that has no say in it. | You are on the rego, you hold the keys, and you still cannot sell the car. That is what most people find the first time they try to get out of a novated lease early. The finance company holds title under the lease, and the arrangement runs between you, your employer and that financier, not with you alone. The registration certificate has no say in it. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | A novated lease is a three-way arrangement. You sign a lease with a finance company, then a deed of novation passes some or all of your obligations under that lease across to your employer, who meets the payments out of your salary. The Australian Taxation Office describes the same tripartite structure in its guidance, and it is why two documents — the lease and the deed — decide what happens next. Being the registered operator on a Queensland record is not the same thing as owning the vehicle. | A novated lease is a three-way arrangement. You sign a lease with a finance company, then a deed of novation passes some or all of your obligations under that lease across to your employer, who meets the payments out of your salary. The Australian Taxation Office describes the same three-way structure in its guidance. It is why two documents, the lease and the deed, decide what happens next. Being the registered operator on a Queensland record is not the same thing as owning the vehicle. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | The first phone call is to the financier, not to a buyer. Ask for a written payout figure showing the amount, any early termination fees, the date it stays valid until and how the security interest gets discharged. Our guide to [selling a financed car in Queensland](/blog/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld) sets out the payout, PPSR and settlement sequence in detail, and the same discipline applies here with one extra party in the chain. Your salary packaging provider can usually pull the figure faster than the financier's call centre. | Call the financier first, not a buyer. Ask for a written payout figure showing the amount, any early termination fees, the date it stays valid until and how the security interest gets discharged. Our guide to [selling a financed car in Queensland](/blog/how-to-sell-a-car-with-finance-owing-qld) sets out the payout, PPSR and settlement steps in detail. The same steps apply here, with one extra party involved. Your salary packaging provider can usually get the figure faster than the financier's call centre. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | Every lease of this kind ends with a residual — a balloon amount owing at the end of the term, set when the lease was written. Who carries it is not a given. The ATO distinguishes a full novation, where the employer takes on the lease payments and guarantees the residual value, from a split full novation, where the residual payment obligation stays with the employee. Find out which one you signed before assuming the balloon is someone else's problem. | Every lease of this kind ends with a residual: a balloon amount owing at the end of the term, set when the lease was written. Who carries it varies. The ATO distinguishes a full novation, where the employer takes on the lease payments and guarantees the residual value, from a split full novation, where the residual payment obligation stays with the employee. Find out which one you signed before you assume the balloon is someone else's problem. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | Ending the lease early usually means paying it out, and a payout early in a term can sit well above what the car would fetch on the open market. That gap is yours to fund. It is the same negative-equity arithmetic a car loan produces, except the figure comes off a lease schedule rather than a shrinking balance. | Ending the lease early usually means paying it out. A payout early in a term can be well above what the car would sell for on the open market. That gap is yours to pay. It is the same negative equity a car loan can produce, except the figure comes off a lease schedule rather than a shrinking balance. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-novated-lease-qld.ts` | When employment finishes, the novation finishes with it and the obligations come back to you, subject to what the deed says. Ask your packaging provider about your options while you are still employed: novating to a new employer, meeting the payments yourself, or paying out and selling. | When employment finishes, the novation finishes with it and the obligations come back to you, subject to what the deed says. | `blog/posts/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld.ts` | A car in a Redcliffe carport, an owner who has moved into aged care, and an adult child holding a document they have never had to use. | `blog/posts/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld.ts` | Queensland recognises two. A general power of attorney covers financial matters and ends if the principal loses capacity. An enduring power of attorney continues after capacity is lost — that is the point of it — and can extend to personal and health matters as well. If the reason the car is being sold is that its owner can no longer manage their own affairs, a general power of attorney is not the document that gets it done. | Queensland recognises two. A general power of attorney covers financial matters and ends if the principal loses capacity. An enduring power of attorney continues after capacity is lost, and can extend to personal and health matters as well. If the car is being sold because its owner can no longer manage their own affairs, a general power of attorney will not do the job. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld.ts` | Read the document before you ring anyone. An enduring power of attorney states when the power for financial matters starts: it can begin immediately, at a stated time or occasion, or only once the principal's capacity is impaired. That single line decides whether you can act today or whether a doctor's assessment comes first, and it is worth finding before a truck is booked. | Read the document before you ring anyone. An enduring power of attorney states when the power for financial matters starts: it can begin immediately, at a stated time or occasion, or only once the principal's capacity is impaired. That one line decides whether you can act today or whether a doctor's assessment comes first. Find it before you book a truck. |
| `blog/posts/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld.ts` | That is a process measured in weeks, so start it before the registration lapses rather than after. | `blog/posts/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld.ts` | One useful thing is settled: an attorney does not need the registered operator's written consent to request a release of vehicle registration information from TMR. | `blog/posts/sell-car-power-of-attorney-qld.ts` | The second is money and records. | `blog/posts/sell-car-without-keys-brisbane.ts` | On many modern vehicles, the cut blade and the electronic authorisation are separate. A van kept under a carport in Kenmore is in very different shape from one that stood uncovered at Redcliffe with the salt air working on the chassis, the coupling and the brake drums. |
| `blog/posts/sell-caravan-brisbane.ts` | Age brings a second problem: asbestos. If an old van has asbestos in it, the council tip is not an option. That closes the obvious disposal route for exactly the vans most likely to need one — [taking a vehicle to the tip in Brisbane](/blog/take-car-to-tip-brisbane) covers what the centres will and will not take. | Age brings a second problem: asbestos. If the van has not moved in years, assume flat tyres, a seized jockey wheel and a drawbar that has settled into a garden bed. | A van is only worth something if someone can get it out of your yard. Older suburbs make that harder than it sounds: a Queenslander on stumps with a narrow side gate, a shared driveway in Toowong, a battle-axe block at Capalaba with one turning circle and a jacaranda planted in the middle of it. If the van has not moved in years, assume flat tyres, a seized jockey wheel and a drawbar that has settled into a garden bed. |

| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | Selling a company car in QLD? Three checks a private sale never has: who may sign for the entity, GST on the sale price, and whether the ACN still exists. | Selling a company car in QLD? Three checks a private sale never has: who can sign for the company, GST on the sale price, and whether the ACN still exists. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | A work ute registered to a Pty Ltd sells the same way any other vehicle does, right up to the moment someone has to sign. Selling a company car in QLD adds three checks a private sale never has: who is authorised to sign for the entity, whether GST applies to the sale price, and whether the company still legally exists. Any one of them can stall a transfer at the counter, and the third one can stall it for months. | A work ute registered to a Pty Ltd sells the same way as any other vehicle, until someone has to sign. Selling a company car in QLD adds three checks a private sale never has: who is authorised to sign for the entity, whether GST applies to the sale price, and whether the company still legally exists. Any one of them can stall a transfer at the counter, and the third one can stall it for months. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | In practice that means arriving with more than the registration certificate. Take the ACN and the authority document. That matters most when the person signing is not an obvious officeholder — a bookkeeper, an adult child winding up a parent's business, a partner who was never on the ASIC record. Everything else about the sale is ordinary; our guide to [Queensland seller paperwork](/blog/what-paperwork-to-sell-a-car-qld) covers the steps that do not change just because the operator is a business. | In practice, bring more than the registration certificate. Take the ACN and the authority document. That matters most when the person signing is not an obvious officeholder, such as a bookkeeper, an adult child winding up a parent's business, or a partner who was never on the ASIC record. The rest of the sale is ordinary. Our guide to [Queensland seller paperwork](/blog/what-paperwork-to-sell-a-car-qld) covers the steps that stay the same when the operator is a business. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | If the entity is registered for GST and the vehicle was a business asset, disposing of it is generally a taxable sale. The ATO's position is that the GST is generally one-eleventh of the sale price, and that it applies even where the buyer is a private individual who is not in business. Plenty of small operators assume GST only attaches to dealer transactions and find out otherwise at the next activity statement. | If the entity is registered for GST and the vehicle was a business asset, disposing of it is generally a taxable sale. The ATO's position is that the GST is generally one-eleventh of the sale price, and that it applies even where the buyer is a private individual who is not in business. Many small operators assume GST only applies to dealer sales and find out otherwise at the next activity statement. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | Two qualifiers matter. A genuinely private asset is different — a sole trader selling a vehicle never used for business, on which no GST credit was claimed, is not making a taxable sale. Where a vehicle was used partly for business and partly privately, a decreasing adjustment may reduce the amount payable. Which applies is an accountant's call rather than a buyer's, so settle it before you agree on a figure. | Two qualifiers matter. A genuinely private asset is different — a sole trader selling a vehicle never used for business, on which no GST credit was claimed, is not making a taxable sale. Where a vehicle was used partly for business and partly privately, a decreasing adjustment may reduce the amount payable. Which one applies is a question for your accountant, not the buyer, so settle it before you agree on a price. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | This is the version that stops people cold. The ute has been sitting behind a shed at Acacia Ridge for two years, the Pty Ltd was deregistered somewhere in that time, and the registration certificate still names an entity that no longer exists. On deregistration, a company's property vests in ASIC, which becomes the party able to deal with it. | This is the case that catches people out. The ute has been sitting behind a shed at Acacia Ridge for two years, the Pty Ltd was deregistered during that time, and the registration certificate still names an entity that no longer exists. On deregistration, a company's property vests in ASIC, which becomes the party able to deal with it. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | ASIC's published position on vehicles is more workable than that sounds. It says the party in possession of a motor vehicle is likely to be the owner, because it is that party rather than ASIC exercising management and control over it, and that registration in the company's name is not by itself sufficient proof of ownership. ASIC also notes it has no knowledge of whether a vehicle was ever company property, or whether the company dealt with it before deregistration. The registration itself still has to be resolved: it needs to be transferred to a legal entity that exists, or cancelled with the state registration authority. | ASIC's published position on vehicles is more practical than that sounds. It says the party in possession of a motor vehicle is likely to be the owner, because it is that party rather than ASIC exercising management and control over it, and that registration in the company's name is not by itself sufficient proof of ownership. ASIC also notes it has no knowledge of whether a vehicle was ever company property, or whether the company dealt with it before deregistration. The registration itself still has to be resolved: it needs to be transferred to a legal entity that exists, or cancelled with the state registration authority. |
| `blog/posts/sell-company-car-qld.ts` | So the car is rarely stuck, but the evidence burden shifts onto you. Expect to show how you came to hold it — purchase invoices, a finance payout letter, insurance in your own name — and take advice from ASIC or your accountant before treating a deregistered company's vehicle as yours to sell. | So the car is rarely stuck, but you have to supply the evidence. Expect to show how you came to hold it, with documents such as purchase invoices, a finance payout letter or insurance in your own name. Take advice from ASIC or your accountant before treating a deregistered company's vehicle as yours to sell. |

Read the details for [selling a van](/blog/sell-van-brisbane) and for [older work utes](/blog/sell-my-ute-brisbane) before you strip anything out. |

| `blog/posts/sell-deceased-estate-car-qld.ts` | Queensland's requirements depend on whether there is a grant, a valid will, or a next-of-kin application. | `blog/posts/sell-deceased-estate-car-qld.ts` | The central form is the Application to Transact with Registration Products on behalf of a Deceased Person (F5296). It is used by an executor, administrator, or eligible next of kin to deal with vehicle registrations and number plates recorded in the deceased person's name. The form and supporting evidence should be checked against the current TMR instructions rather than an informal document list. | The main form is the Application to Transact with Registration Products on behalf of a Deceased Person (F5296). An executor, administrator, or eligible next of kin uses it to deal with vehicle registrations and number plates recorded in the deceased person's name. Check the form and supporting evidence against the current TMR instructions, not an informal document list. |
| `blog/posts/sell-deceased-estate-car-qld.ts` | Where there is no grant but there is a valid will, an executor named in the will may be able to proceed by providing the original or a certified copy of the will and completing F5296, including its statutory declaration. Where there is no will, grant, or letters of administration, an eligible next of kin may be able to use the F5296 next-of-kin pathway and statutory declaration. Disputed estates or uncertainty about who may act should be resolved with TMR and appropriate estate advice before the vehicle is sold. | Where there is no grant but there is a valid will, an executor named in the will may be able to proceed by providing the original or a certified copy of the will and completing F5296, including its statutory declaration. Where there is no will, grant, or letters of administration, an eligible next of kin may be able to use the F5296 next-of-kin pathway and statutory declaration. If the estate is disputed or it is unclear who may act, resolve that with TMR and appropriate estate advice before the vehicle is sold. |
| `blog/posts/sell-deceased-estate-car-qld.ts` | Before requesting quotes, record the make, model, year, VIN, registration status, kilometres, condition, keys, location, and access. | `blog/posts/sell-fire-damaged-car-brisbane.ts` | Fire damage rarely stops at the panel where it started. Here is how a Brisbane owner works out what is left of the car's value, and what has to be settled before it can be collected. | Fire damage rarely stops at the panel where it started. Here is how to work out what a fire-damaged car is still worth in Brisbane, and what to settle before pickup. |
| `blog/posts/sell-fire-damaged-car-brisbane.ts` | A car fire almost never stops where it started. An engine-bay fire in a Moorooka carport takes the wiring loom, the plastics under the bonnet and the paint on the guards before anyone reaches it with an extinguisher, and what is left is a fire-damaged car worth nothing like the one insured the month before. It still has a clear path out of the driveway. That path just runs through the insurer, TMR and a tilt tray rather than a private buyer. | A car fire almost never stops where it started. An engine-bay fire in a Moorooka carport takes the wiring loom, the plastics under the bonnet and the paint on the guards before anyone reaches it with an extinguisher. What is left is a fire-damaged car worth far less than the one insured the month before. There is still a clear way out of the driveway. It runs through the insurer, TMR and a tilt tray, not a private buyer. |
| `blog/posts/sell-fire-damaged-car-brisbane.ts` | Heat moves through a car unevenly. A fire contained to the engine bay can leave the rear half of the shell, the boot floor, the tailgate, the glass and the rear suspension intact and saleable. A fire that reaches the cabin usually takes the interior, the airbags and the loom with it. Anything that sat in or beside flame is suspect from then on: restraint components, seatbelt pretensioners and alloy wheels that have been through a hot fire are not parts a careful buyer puts back into circulation. | Heat moves through a car unevenly. A fire contained to the engine bay can leave the rear half of the shell, the boot floor, the tailgate, the glass and the rear suspension intact and saleable. A fire that reaches the cabin usually takes the interior, the airbags and the loom with it. Anything that sat in or beside flame is suspect from then on. A careful buyer will not reuse restraint components, seatbelt pretensioners or alloy wheels that have been through a hot fire. |
| `blog/posts/sell-fire-damaged-car-brisbane.ts` | So a burnt car gets quoted more like a scrap job with a few surviving assemblies than a used car with a fault. Past that, what a fire-damaged car is worth comes down to which assemblies the fire never reached, which is why photographs beat descriptions here. | So a burnt car is quoted more like a scrap job with a few surviving parts than a used car with a fault. Weight still counts, because the steel and aluminium are recycled like any end-of-life vehicle. [How car recycling works in Australia](/blog/how-car-recycling-works-australia) covers what happens after pickup. Beyond that, what a fire-damaged car is worth comes down to which parts the fire never reached. That is why photos tell a buyer more than a description. |

| `blog/posts/sell-hybrid-electric-car-brisbane.ts` | Almost no electric vehicle sold in Australia is approved for flat towing with all four wheels rolling on the road. When the wheels turn, the motor acts as a generator and pushes current back into a system that is not managing it, which can damage the motor, inverter, and pack—and on most models it voids the powertrain warranty. Manufacturers overwhelmingly specify transport with the driven wheels off the ground, which in practice means a tilt tray rather than a dolly. | Almost no electric vehicle sold in Australia is approved for flat towing with all four wheels rolling on the road. When the wheels turn, the motor acts as a generator and pushes current back into a system that is not managing it. That can damage the motor, inverter, and pack, and on most models it voids the powertrain warranty. Manufacturers overwhelmingly specify transport with the driven wheels off the ground. In practice that means a tilt tray, not a dolly. |
| `blog/posts/sell-hybrid-electric-car-brisbane.ts` | Brisbane's housing stock is where that requirement bites. | `blog/posts/sell-imported-car-brisbane.ts` | The badge on the tailgate is rarely what holds these sales up — the paper trail behind it is. Find that evidence before you list, not after someone asks for it. |
| `blog/posts/sell-imported-car-brisbane.ts` | Vehicles brought into Australia before 1 July 2021 were fitted with an identification plate — the compliance plate people go looking for in the door jamb or under the bonnet. From that date the Commonwealth Road Vehicle Standards Act replaced that plate with an entry on the Register of Approved Vehicles, a national database of vehicles approved for supply to the Australian market. It is publicly searchable, so a buyer can check the vehicle themselves, which is the reason to check it yourself first. | Vehicles brought into Australia before 1 July 2021 were fitted with an identification plate — the compliance plate people go looking for in the door jamb or under the bonnet. From that date the Commonwealth Road Vehicle Standards Act replaced that plate with an entry on the Register of Approved Vehicles, a national database of vehicles approved for supply to the Australian market. It is publicly searchable, so a buyer can check the vehicle themselves. Check it yourself first. |
| `blog/posts/sell-imported-car-brisbane.ts` | Age matters to the pathway. A vehicle built more than 25 years ago can be entered on the register through a concessional RAV entry approval under the older-vehicles criterion, and that approval is also taken to be the import approval for the car. A Delica or an early Skyline that came in that way has a documented history. One with no plate, no register entry and no approval on file has a gap, and the person buying it will price that gap in. | Age matters to the pathway. A vehicle built more than 25 years ago can be entered on the register through a concessional RAV entry approval under the older-vehicles criterion, and that approval is also taken to be the import approval for the car. A Delica or an early Skyline that came in that way has a documented history. One with no plate, no register entry and no approval on file has a gap, and the buyer will allow for that gap in the price. |
| `blog/posts/sell-imported-car-brisbane.ts` | Most vehicles built after 1989 carry a 17-character VIN, and most built before that carry a chassis number instead. A search run against the wrong identifier is not the reassurance either party thinks it is. | Most vehicles built after 1989 carry a 17-character VIN, and most built before that carry a chassis number instead. A search on the wrong identifier does not give either side the reassurance they think it does. |

Queensland's 14-day rule, what re-registering costs, and when selling an interstate-registered car is cheaper. |
| `blog/posts/sell-interstate-registered-car-brisbane.ts` | The car followed you up the highway and the interstate rego is running down. Queensland wants the plates and a safety certificate before it issues new ones — here's how that maths usually falls. | You moved north and the interstate rego is running down. Queensland wants the old plates and a safety certificate before it issues new ones. Here is how the costs usually compare. |
| `blog/posts/sell-interstate-registered-car-brisbane.ts` | Queensland gives you 14 days. Move here with a vehicle that will be garaged at a Queensland address and Transport and Main Roads expects it registered in Queensland within a fortnight, with fines available if you let it drift. That deadline is what forces the question for a lot of new arrivals: pay to bring the car across, or sell an interstate-registered car in Brisbane and stop paying rego to a state you no longer live in. | Queensland gives you 14 days. Move here with a vehicle that will be garaged at a Queensland address and Transport and Main Roads expects it registered in Queensland within a fortnight, with fines available if you let it drift. That deadline forces the question for many new arrivals: pay to bring the car across, or sell an interstate-registered car in Brisbane and stop paying rego to a state you no longer live in. |
| `blog/posts/sell-interstate-registered-car-brisbane.ts` | The rego doesn't just port across at the counter. To register here you hand your interstate number plates in and produce a current Queensland safety certificate, then finish the registration at a transport and motoring customer service centre. Only an Approved Inspection Station can issue that certificate. There are stations right across Brisbane, but an inspection done in Lismore or Tweed Heads on the way up doesn't count for anything here. | The rego doesn't just carry over at the counter. To register here you hand your interstate number plates in and produce a current Queensland safety certificate, then finish the registration at a transport and motoring customer service centre. Only an Approved Inspection Station can issue that certificate. There are stations right across Brisbane, but an inspection done in Lismore or Tweed Heads on the way up doesn't count for anything here. |
| `blog/posts/sell-interstate-registered-car-brisbane.ts` | The 14 days are meant as a working window rather than a countdown. Our guide to [getting a safety certificate in Brisbane](/blog/how-to-get-a-roadworthy-certificate-brisbane) covers what inspectors actually check. | The 14 days are meant as a working window rather than a countdown. | `blog/posts/sell-lpg-car-brisbane.ts` | Queensland treats a gas system as a separate inspection item, with its own inspector and its own expiry clock. The tank date, the buyer's questions, and whether the certificate is worth chasing at all all follow from it. | Queensland treats a gas system as a separate inspection item, with its own inspector and its own expiry clock. | `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | What your bike is actually worth, what sets the price, and the QLD paperwork — how to sell a motorbike in Brisbane without the runaround. | How to sell a motorbike in Brisbane: what your bike is worth, what sets the price, and the QLD paperwork you need. |
| `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | Bikes don't price like cars. There's almost no metal in one, so the money sits in the model, the parts and the condition — here's how that plays out in Brisbane. | Bikes don't price like cars. There's almost no metal in one, so the value is in the model, the parts and the condition. Here's how that works in Brisbane. |
| `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | A bike that hasn't turned over since last winter is usually worth more than the owner assumes and less than the classifieds imply. That gap is where the frustration starts when people try to sell a motorbike in Brisbane. Bikes don't price like cars, and the reason is simple: there's almost no metal in one. Whatever you're offered is being paid for the model, the parts and the condition — not the weight. | A bike that hasn't turned over since last winter is usually worth more than the owner thinks and less than the classifieds suggest. That gap is what frustrates people who try to sell a motorbike in Brisbane. Bikes don't price like cars, because there's almost no metal in one. An offer is for the model, the parts and the condition, not the weight. |
| `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | Start with the scales, because the number surprises people. A Commodore heading to a yard has well over a tonne of steel behind its offer, and [what a scrap car is worth in Brisbane](/blog/how-much-is-scrap-car-worth-brisbane) is essentially a weight calculation. A mid-capacity road bike runs somewhere around 180 to 220 kilograms wet, and a good share of that is aluminium, plastic fairings and a fuel tank. Take it to a metal buyer and you're arguing over a rounding error. Take it to someone who wants the engine, the wheels and the loom, and it's a different conversation entirely. | Start with the weight, because the number surprises people. A Commodore going to a yard has well over a tonne of steel behind its offer, and [what a scrap car is worth in Brisbane](/blog/how-much-is-scrap-car-worth-brisbane) is mostly a weight calculation. A mid-capacity road bike weighs around 180 to 220 kilograms wet, and a good share of that is aluminium, plastic fairings and a fuel tank. A metal buyer will pay almost nothing for it. Someone who wants the engine, the wheels and the loom will see it very differently. |
| `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | Model demand does most of the work. Brisbane has a constant stream of learner riders coming through, so LAMS-approved bikes — the CB125E, the Ninja 400, the MT-07 LAMS, the CFMoto 300s — hold value even when they're tired, because someone always needs one to get across town to a shift at Bowen Hills. Older sports bikes are the opposite. The resale market for them thinned out years ago, but the fairings, forks, wheels and clocks all have buyers waiting, so they're worth real money in pieces. | Demand for the model matters most. Brisbane always has new learner riders, so LAMS-approved bikes (the CB125E, the Ninja 400, the MT-07 LAMS, the CFMoto 300s) hold value even when they're worn, because someone always needs one to get across town to a shift at Bowen Hills. Older sports bikes are the opposite. Their resale market thinned out years ago, but the fairings, forks, wheels and clocks all have buyers, so they're worth real money in parts. |
| `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | Condition is the rest of it, and bikes are unforgiving about details. What costs you is anything suggesting the bike went down hard: a bent subframe, cracked cases, a chewed swingarm. | Condition is the rest of it, and on a bike the details count. Matching keys, an engine that turns over, straight forks, unbent bars and a frame nobody has welded put you at the top of the range. | `blog/posts/sell-motorbike-brisbane.ts` | Brisbane adds two local factors. | `blog/posts/sell-truck-brisbane.ts` | Almost everything about an old truck sale in Queensland hangs off one number: the gross vehicle mass on the compliance plate. Find that plate before you do anything else. | Almost everything about selling an old truck in Queensland depends on one number: the gross vehicle mass on the compliance plate. Under 4,500 kilograms, you sell a truck in Brisbane on much the same terms as a van. Over it, the inspection changes, the licence needed to drive it away changes, and so do the rules about leaving it parked out the front. Find that plate before you do anything else. |
| `blog/posts/sell-truck-brisbane.ts` | It usually sits inside the driver's door frame or on the firewall, and the figure you want is GVM rather than tare or GCM. | `blog/posts/sell-truck-brisbane.ts` | A Queensland class C licence covers vehicles up to 4.5 tonnes GVM. | `blog/posts/tow-truck-access-brisbane.ts` | Three things have to work at once: room to position the truck, a reasonably straight path to move the car onto the deck, and clearance above both. | `blog/posts/tow-truck-access-brisbane.ts` | Pre-war Queenslanders through Paddington, Red Hill and Ashgrove are the classic problem: a short steep drive down to an under-house garage, a hard turn at the bottom, and timber stumps or a low bearer at the entry. The car frequently has to be brought up to the street before anything can be loaded, and that pull needs its own clear run. | Pre-war Queenslanders through Paddington, Red Hill and Ashgrove are the classic problem: a short steep drive down to an under-house garage, a hard turn at the bottom, and timber stumps or a low bearer at the entry. | `blog/posts/tow-truck-access-brisbane.ts` | Post-war blocks around Chermside, Wavell Heights and Moorooka trade the slope for overhead obstructions — carport beams, later-added awnings, a low gutter line and a mature poinciana over the drive. Battle-axe blocks in The Gap and Ferny Hills bring a long single-lane shared drive with nowhere to turn around, which usually means the truck reverses the full length and needs the whole drive clear of bins, trailers and the neighbour's second car. | Post-war blocks around Chermside, Wavell Heights and Moorooka have less slope but more overhead: carport beams, later-added awnings, a low gutter line and a mature poinciana over the drive. That usually means the truck reverses the full length and needs the whole drive clear of bins, trailers and the neighbour's second car. |
| `blog/posts/tow-truck-access-brisbane.ts` | Apartments in West End, Newstead and South Brisbane are their own category. | `blog/posts/tow-truck-cost-brisbane.ts` | Queensland caps the charge on some tows and leaves others entirely to the market. Which one you're in decides whether you're up for a few hundred dollars or nothing at all. | Queensland caps the charge on some tows and leaves others to the market. Which type of tow you need decides whether you pay a few hundred dollars or nothing at all. |
| `blog/posts/tow-truck-cost-brisbane.ts` | Ring three operators about the same job and you'll get three different numbers, because tow truck cost in Brisbane depends less on the truck than on why the car needs moving. Queensland caps what you can be charged for some tows and leaves others entirely to the market. Working out which category you're in is worth more than any average price — it's the difference between a few hundred dollars and nothing at all. | Ring three operators about the same job and you'll get three different prices, because tow truck cost in Brisbane depends less on the truck than on why the car needs moving. Queensland caps what you can be charged for some tows and leaves others to the market. Knowing which category you're in matters more than any average price. It's the difference between a few hundred dollars and nothing at all. |
| `blog/posts/tow-truck-cost-brisbane.ts` | Queensland's tow truck scheme runs on the Tow Truck Act 2023 and the Tow Truck Regulation 2024. It covers crash-related towing, police-seized vehicles taken from off-street regulated parking areas, and the towing of vehicles from private property — but only inside declared regulated areas. Greater Brisbane sits inside one. What the scheme does not regulate is breakdown towing or trade towing, which is the category most people are actually in when the car won't start in the driveway at Wynnum. | Queensland's tow truck scheme runs on the Tow Truck Act 2023 and the Tow Truck Regulation 2024. It covers crash-related towing, police-seized vehicles taken from off-street regulated parking areas, and the towing of vehicles from private property — but only inside declared regulated areas. Greater Brisbane sits inside one. What the scheme does not regulate is breakdown towing or trade towing, which is the category most people are in when the car won't start in the driveway at Wynnum. |
| `blog/posts/tow-truck-cost-brisbane.ts` | That split is the whole story. A crash tow off the Gateway Motorway falls under a maximum charge set in the regulation, so there is a published ceiling. A breakdown tow from Old Cleveland Road to your mechanic has no ceiling at all — the price is whatever you agree to before the truck is dispatched. Transport and Main Roads publishes the current maximum charges, and they're adjusted periodically, so ask for the figure rather than assuming a quote already reflects it. Regulated areas are mapped rather than described in words: the Queensland Globe shows them if you add the transportation layer and tick tow truck regulated areas. Go far enough west or north of the city and you leave the scheme behind entirely. | That split explains the price. A crash tow off the Gateway Motorway falls under a maximum charge set in the regulation, so there is a published ceiling. A breakdown tow from Old Cleveland Road to your mechanic has no ceiling at all. The price is whatever you agree to before the truck is dispatched. Transport and Main Roads publishes the current maximum charges, and they're adjusted periodically, so ask for the figure rather than assuming a quote already reflects it. Regulated areas are shown on a map, not described in words: the Queensland Globe shows them if you add the transportation layer and tick tow truck regulated areas. Go far enough west or north of the city and you are outside the scheme. |
| `blog/posts/tow-truck-cost-brisbane.ts` | Inside a regulated area you have the right to decide who tows your vehicle and where it goes. That matters at the roadside, where the first truck to turn up is not automatically the one you're obliged to use, and where nobody is at their most decisive. | Inside a regulated area you have the right to decide who tows your vehicle and where it goes. That matters at the roadside, where it is hard to think clearly. You do not have to use the first truck that turns up. |
| `blog/posts/tow-truck-cost-brisbane.ts` | With no cap in play, tow truck cost in Brisbane comes down to a call-out fee plus distance, adjusted for everything that makes the job harder. | `blog/posts/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld.ts` | A toll notice in Queensland goes to whoever the vehicle is registered to when the trip happened — not the person who was driving, and not the person holding the keys today. That's the trap with unpaid tolls when selling a car: liability sits with the registration, and the registration doesn't change the instant money changes hands. Queensland Transport says it plainly on its selling-a-used-vehicle page — you remain liable for tolls and fines for a vehicle registered in your name. | A toll notice in Queensland goes to whoever the vehicle is registered to when the trip happened. Queensland Transport says it plainly on its selling-a-used-vehicle page: you remain liable for tolls and fines for a vehicle registered in your name. |
| `blog/posts/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld.ts` | Brisbane gives that plenty of chances to bite. The Gateway and Logan motorways and AirportlinkM7 run under concession agreements with the state, while the Clem7, Legacy Way, and the Go Between Bridge run under agreements with Brisbane City Council. All of them are operated by Transurban Queensland and billed through Linkt, and none of them have booths. A gantry reads the plate, and the charge lands wherever that plate is attached. | Brisbane has plenty of toll roads where that can happen. The Gateway and Logan motorways and AirportlinkM7 run under concession agreements with the state, while the Clem7, Legacy Way, and the Go Between Bridge run under agreements with Brisbane City Council. All of them are operated by Transurban Queensland and billed through Linkt, and none of them have booths. A gantry reads the plate, and the charge lands wherever that plate is attached. |
| `blog/posts/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld.ts` | Most unpaid tolls when selling a car land in the gap between handover and transfer. In Queensland the buyer has 14 days to transfer the registration into their name, and Transport and Main Roads recommends that the seller help by transferring it out of their own name. Until the registration has left your name, the vehicle is still recorded against you. Some buyers take the full fortnight. Some never lodge it at all. Every tolled trip in that window is billed to the old registration, which is why completing the seller's side of the [transfer of ownership](/blog/how-to-transfer-car-ownership-qld) on the day — and keeping the confirmation — matters more than any other step in this article. | Most unpaid tolls when selling a car land in the gap between handover and transfer. In Queensland the buyer has 14 days to transfer the registration into their name, and Transport and Main Roads recommends that the seller help by transferring it out of their own name. Until the registration has left your name, the vehicle is still recorded against you. Some buyers take the full fortnight. Some never lodge it at all. Every tolled trip in that window is billed to the old registration. That is why completing the seller's side of the [transfer of ownership](/blog/how-to-transfer-car-ownership-qld) on the day, and keeping the confirmation, matters more than any other step in this article. |
| `blog/posts/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld.ts` | Fixing it takes two minutes. Log in to your Linkt account, open Vehicles, and remove the car by its plate number. Do it the day the vehicle leaves rather than the weekend after. While you're in the housekeeping mood, cancel any direct debit paying the registration — Queensland Transport flags that one on the same page, and a rego payment leaving your account for a car you no longer own is an irritating way to find out you forgot. | Fixing it takes two minutes. Log in to your Linkt account, open Vehicles, and remove the car by its plate number. Do it the day the vehicle leaves, not the weekend after. While you're at it, cancel any direct debit paying the registration. Queensland Transport flags that one on the same page, and a rego payment leaving your account for a car you no longer own is an annoying way to find out you forgot. |
| `blog/posts/unpaid-tolls-selling-car-qld.ts` | Do not infer the registration path from labels such as wrecker or cash-for-cars buyer. If the vehicle is being sold for parts, Queensland says it must be deregistered before sale regardless of the buyer's label. If it is not a for-parts sale, confirm whether it is already unregistered or whether a registered transfer or direct licensed-dealer acquisition applies. Complete the applicable step and remove the sold vehicle from Linkt so later plate-based trips are not charged to your account. | Do not work out the registration path from a label such as wrecker or cash-for-cars buyer. If the vehicle is being sold for parts, Queensland says it must be deregistered before sale regardless of the buyer's label. If it is not a for-parts sale, confirm whether it is already unregistered or whether a registered transfer or direct licensed-dealer acquisition applies. Complete the applicable step and remove the sold vehicle from Linkt so later plate-based trips are not charged to your account. |

| `blog/posts/wovr-written-off-vehicle-register-qld-guide.ts` | If your car has been in a serious prang, caught in a flood, or written off by the insurer, you've probably been told it's now on the WOVR — the Written-Off Vehicle Register. | `blog/posts/wovr-written-off-vehicle-register-qld-guide.ts` | The WOVR classification has a massive impact on what the car is worth at resale. A statutory write-off is worth what its parts and scrap metal weigh. A repairable write-off, on the other hand, retains significant value as a rebuild project or as a donor vehicle for other cars of the same make and model — and that value can be surprisingly good for popular models like Hilux, Ranger, Commodore, Camry, and Corolla where parts demand across Queensland never drops. | The WOVR classification makes a big difference to what the car is worth. A statutory write-off is worth its parts and scrap metal. A repairable write-off keeps more value as a rebuild project or as a donor vehicle for other cars of the same make and model. That value can be higher for popular models like Hilux, Ranger, Commodore, Camry and Corolla, where parts are in steady demand across Queensland. |
| `blog/posts/wovr-written-off-vehicle-register-qld-guide.ts` | For sellers, the key question is whether you can still sell the car once it has been written off. The process depends on the classification. A statutory write-off cannot be re-registered and is generally sold for parts or scrap. A repairable write-off may be sold to a buyer who understands the inspection and re-registration requirements, a rebuilder or mechanic, or a [damaged-car buyer](/damaged-cars-brisbane). | For sellers, the main question is whether you can still sell the car once it has been written off. That depends on the classification. A statutory write-off cannot be re-registered and is generally sold for parts or scrap. A repairable write-off may be sold to a buyer who understands the inspection and re-registration requirements, a rebuilder or mechanic, or a [damaged-car buyer](/damaged-cars-brisbane). |
